## 14. Slices

**Tier** is the review tier of `next-ticket.md` step 1b: LOW, STD or HIGH. Every slice ships dark or in preview. Migration numbers are the next free number at build time (R1).

### Group A — The pane and the package

| Id | What | Done when | Tier | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A1** | 🔨 **BUILT (2026-11-02).** The Studio pane at `/studio`. It was a preview pane. It is live in the nav since 2026-11-05 (D179, task 1795), and its routes still need `STUDIO_ORGS`. One library lists apps (`apps`), workflows (`workflows`) and skills (F1, when it exists). A kind filter reads `?kind=`. Module Studio is a tab. The create, fork, pin and delete flows of apps and the create, duplicate and delete flows of workflows move in. `/workflows`, `/build/apps` and `/build/agents` redirect to it. `nav.ts` holds one preview pane in place of three | The old routes answer 308 to `/studio`. The run page and the Workshop under `/build/apps/{slug}` stay. A `nav.test.ts` case held `/studio` as the one preview pane. It now holds it as live (D179). The mirrors move too: `SHELL_PANES` in `me.py`, `PREVIEW_BAR_HOME` and `app-title-bar.spec.ts`, and `runActivity.test.ts`. The library shows each kind with its owner and state. Apps show only to a member who holds `build.apps`. Visual review in four contexts | STD | AGENT-SAFE |
| **A2** | 🔨 **BUILT (2026-11-01).** Enum tokens are camelCase (`longText`, `multiSelect`, `mindMap`, `processFlow`), as the genUI node types are, and keys stay snake_case. Caps: depth 20, a string of 20,000 characters, a list of 500 items, a document of 256 KiB. The validator never raises on any JSON value. Credential shapes come from one list, `acb_common/secret_shapes.py` (J5 points the older copies at it). The package manifest schema v1 and its one validator: `packages/acb_common/acb_common/studio_manifest.py` (pydantic, pure data, no gateway import), the committed export `studio_manifest.schema.json`, and hand-written TypeScript types in `workbench/control_plane/src/lib/studio/manifest.ts` held equal by a parity test (no code generator, as the genUI lockstep does). `validate_manifest(doc, *, catalog=None)` takes the catalog as an input: a map of action id to class. With no catalog it checks structure only. **The `uses` grammar:** `object:<name>.(list|get|create|update|delete)`, `<app>.<op>`, `integration:<action>` or `workflow:<uuid>` (B1's ids). An `object:` action is checked against the package's own objects. **Not yet in v1:** `rules`, a state machine (C6), a formula or roll-up (C7) and a code block (L1) are reserved keys, and each refusal names the slice that lifts it. A logic module (B4) needs no key: it is a catalog action | Each refusal of §3.7 has a test that fails with a JSON path and one sentence: an unknown block, an action the catalog does not serve, a class X action, a colour, a pixel size, a credential-shaped string, a field that a view names and the object lacks, an undeclared submit, skill action or card, a bad relation, a duplicate id, a select with no options. The §3.6 example validates with no catalog and with a fake one. The committed JSON Schema equals the live export. `test_studio_manifest_ts_parity.py` holds the TypeScript types equal | STD | AGENT-SAFE |
| **A3** | 🔨 **BUILT, dark (2026-11-01).** Migration `322_apps_format.sql`. The package store: `apps` gains `format` only, because `apps.manifest` exists since 114. `format` is nullable with DEFAULT `code`, so an old row reads as `code` with no backfill. A reader takes NULL as `code` too. **Two CHECKs:** `apps_format` holds `code` and `manifest`. `apps_manifest_package` needs a package to hold an object with a number `studio`, in at most 262144 bytes of jsonb text. **A trigger**, `apps_format_fixed`, refuses each change of `format`, so a code app never becomes a package that skipped A2. **The writer** is `gateway/studio/packages_store.py`: `create_package`, `get_package`, `list_packages` and `update_manifest`, each over a session of `tenant_session(org)` and for one member. It runs `validate_manifest` before each write, then checks the JSON text itself. A new package is private, a draft, and has no files. **The slug** is `<manifest id>-<8 hex digits of the new id>`, because `apps.slug` is unique for all orgs. A taken slug writes nothing, and the store takes a new id. **Five readers skip a package:** `list_apps`, `load_app` (so each slug route is 404), `list_pinned_apps`, `orchestrator/app_tools.py` and `_app_workspace_for` of `routes/workspace.py`. `Path('')` is the working directory, so `_common.workspace_dir` refuses an empty or blank `workspace_path` too, for each reader of the column (fix round 1). `update_manifest` refuses a new manifest id, because the slug holds it. No route, no UI and no publish. **For A4:** `app_versions` needs no new column. A version writes `bundle_html = ''` and `bundle_sha256` as the SHA-256 of the manifest JSON, because both columns are NOT NULL. **Later:** a slug key for each org is a contract step of its own | An expand-only migration (R6). R8: org B reads none of A's packages. `test_tenant_coverage.py` passes. `test_studio_packages_r8.py` (`pytestmark = _DB_GATE`): org B and an unbound session read none of org A's packages, and a member reads only their own. An INSERT of the old shape reads as `code`. Each CHECK refuses its case, and a package of exactly the store's limit passes. The format never changes, also for the superuser. An invalid manifest writes nothing. A slug clash with another org leaks nothing. The five readers skip a package and still return a code app. A new manifest id is refused. A source scan finds no other writer of a package. `test_studio_packages_store.py` (no database): validate before write, the size cap and the slug shape | HIGH | AGENT-SAFE |
| **A4** | 🔨 **BUILT, dark (2026-11-01).** No route, no flag and no UI reach it, as with A3 and C1. No migration: every column exists. **Publish, version and roll back**, over `app_versions`. **The install (its scope, its end date and its removal) is WS-PKG** (`package_host.md`, D147). The organization share is J2. The review step is J2 too. **Built** in `gateway/studio/packages_store.py`, the one writer: `publish_version(db, member, package_id, notes, *, catalog)`, `rollback(db, member, package_id, version, *, catalog)`, `list_versions` and `get_version`. The caller passes `catalog` as `{id: class}` from `routes/studio/actions.catalog_rows`, because a store must not import a route. A missing package or version answers None. A rollback to the live version raises. Rollback runs the same catalog checks on the copied manifest, and a refused rollback writes nothing. A `catalog` of None is refused. **Audit:** an `INSERT INTO app_audit` (kind `publish` or `rollback`) in the same transaction. Only the owner of the package reaches it, as in B2a. **The class** comes from `OBJECT_OP_CLASSES` and the declaration from the manifest, so this slice does not need B1c. **The final order of the checks:** the switch, the shape of the action, the app id, the package, the declaration, the class and the approval, as in B2a. Each one runs before the door reads a byte of the body, so a non-owner and a refused action read nothing. Then the body: the door reads `request.stream()` with no session open, because a wait on a slow client must not hold a pool connection. The read stops past `MAX_BODY_BYTES` (1 MiB), and the answer is 413 `body_too_large`. B2c may raise the cap for writes. Then the second check, in a new session: `_check` runs again, so the door reads the package as this member, then checks the declaration and the class on that fresh manifest. **The reason:** a slow client can pass the first check and hold the read open. Without the second check, it reads after it lost access or after the package changed. The owner-only rule hides this today, but J2 (sharing) and C8 (row rules) build on this door. A miss gets the same 404, and a declaration that is gone gets the same 403. An action that now names another source gets 403 `source_not_open`. Then the executor, in that session. **The input:** an empty body means `{}`. The door accepts only the keys of `studio_query.list_input_schema()`: filter, sort, limit and offset. Any other key gets 422. `limit` and `offset` must be an `int` and not a `bool`. The door checks this before the store, because `json.loads` takes `Infinity` and `paging.bounds` raises `OverflowError` on it. An `int` out of range takes the rule of `paging.bounds`, and the answer holds the values that it used. **The answer** has the shape of `RecordPage`: `records`, `total`, `total_at_least`, `offset`, `limit` and `line`. Each record has the shape of the `object:*.get` answer. An object that the package lacks, and None from the store, get 404 `object_missing`. An `ObjectStoreError` gets the same 404, because the body names no id, so a stored value caused it. **The errors:** the door catches `QueryError` before `ObjectStoreError`. Five codes get 422 `{code, message, field}`. A refused body gets the same shape with `query_value_invalid` and no field, and the door reads nothing for it. `query_timeout` gets 503 `{code, message}` with `Retry-After: 2`. The session stays usable after a refusal, and the audit row is still written. The row holds the code and no record data. **The audit:** each served or refused call writes one row. A raw fault writes none, because the session is not safe to write on. ⚠️ **The known limit:** a read holds a pool connection for up to 5 seconds. This is advisory while the surface is owner only and dark. A wider surface needs a cap of reads before it opens. **Not built:** the door op (B2b-2), an integration (B2b-3) and each write (B2c) | `test_studio_action_door_r8.py`, as the app role: the owner lists a filtered, sorted page with the right total, and one audit row lands in the bound org. Another member and org B get 404. An object of another package gets 404 `object_missing`. Each of the six `QueryError` codes maps as stated. An extra key, `limit: true` and `limit: Infinity` get 422 and read nothing. A body past the cap gets 413, and the read stops near the cap. A non-owner, a refused action and an org outside the switch read no byte. No connection of the app role is in a transaction during the read. A package that goes, an owner that changes or a declaration that changes during the read gives 404 or 403, and the door reads no record. After a refusal the session still writes the audit row. Class B and C still get 403 `approval_required`. A class A door op, integration or workflow still gets 403 `source_not_open`. `test_studio_action_door.py` (no database): `OPEN_EXECUTORS` equals exactly the object get and the object list, and the accepted keys equal the properties of `list_input_schema()`. `test_one_writer_of_the_four_tables` keeps SQL out of `invoke.py`. Each mutation of the suite docstring turns a named test red | HIGH | AGENT-SAFE |

Only the MCP edge runs a door op: `agent_edge/tools.py::_door_send` builds the call with `app_manifests.build_call` and sends it over HTTP with the member's credentials. A run inside the gateway needs an ASGI loopback that forwards the caller's session, or a direct handler call. Each one is a new seam at the HIGH tier. The slice refuses class B and C, the `off_door` rows and each op whose route needs a body key outside `build_call` | This spec records the executor choice before the build. | **B2c-1** | 🔨 **BUILT, dark (2026-11-03).** **Direct writes** (§20.4). A member's own act in the app's screen is the approval: a form Save, an inline edit, a board move. The door opens the create, the update and the delete of an object for a DIRECT call, with no card. **Not in this slice:** delegated writes (B2c-2), the door op, integrations, workflows, a batch route (E8), the UI calls (D2b) and the page confirm of a delete (D2b). **The proof.** The orchestrator runs inside the gateway process, so two tokens cannot tell the callers apart. The proof is a signed header, `X-Studio-Direct`, that only the workbench BFF signs. The key is `STUDIO_DIRECT_KEY`, in the gateway `.env` and in the workbench `.env.local`, with the settings field `studio_direct_key`. A key under 32 bytes, or equal to `GATEWAY_INTERNAL_TOKEN` or `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY`, counts as unset. Then no call is direct. The header is `v1.UNIX_SECONDS.HEX_HMAC_SHA256` over the time, the lowercase member email, POST and the gateway path. The window is 60 s, with 5 s of skew. `packages/acb_auth/acb_auth/direct_proof.py` verifies it with `compare_digest`. The door checks it once, before the body read (`invoke._is_direct`). A call is direct only if the proof holds AND `user.agent` is None AND the user is a member whose email has an `@` and does not start with `system:`. A missing or bad proof gives the same 403 `approval_required` as before, so the door gives no oracle. **The door.** `DIRECT_EXECUTORS` holds object create, update and delete. `OPEN_EXECUTORS` stays get and list. The bodies are `{data}` for a create, `{id, data}` for an update and `{id}` for a delete, and no other key (422). One `apply_writes` call with one `Write` runs the change. The store refuses a computed key. **One transaction.** `record_app_audit_in(db, ...)` in `routes/apps/_common.py` writes the `app_audit` row in the session of the write and raises on failure. A failed audit therefore fails the write. The row names its record in `record_id`. A create gives its new id, and an update or a delete gives the UUID of its body. A refusal writes its audit row, leaves the session, and only then answers, because an `HTTPException` inside the session would roll the row back. **Writers.** The door admits at most 3 writes in flight for each org, before it takes a session. The first writer of an org always gets a slot. A second or later writer also needs fewer than 6 in all, so two busy orgs cannot shut out a third. A call past the cap gets 503 `store_busy` with `Retry-After: 1` and holds no connection. The count lives in the process. The gateway runs one uvicorn worker, so it holds for the box. The door catches `StoreBusy` before `ObjectStoreError` and answers 503 with `Retry-After: 1`. A rule-check timeout is 503 with `Retry-After: 2`. **Refusals.** HTTP 422 with `{code, message, rule, fieldErrors}`. `FieldError` gives `field_invalid` and a `fieldErrors` entry for the key. Each `RuleViolation` (`ConstraintViolation`, `MoveViolation`, `FormulaError`) gives `rule_refused`, the `rule` and the message. A write to a formula target gives `computed_field`. `FormulaError` gained an optional `field`, so `fieldErrors` names the target. `RecordError` and any other store refusal give `record_invalid`. A body of the wrong shape gives `bad_input`. A missing record is 404. **BFF.** `/api/studio` signs the proof from `lib/gateway.ts` (`studioDirectHeaders`) for a POST to `apps/ID/actions/ACTION` only. It signs only a request from a page of its own origin (`sameOrigin`), because the session cookie is `SameSite=Lax`. A POST from another origin goes on unsigned and gets 403. It forwards `Retry-After`. It adds no other header and no identity. **Delete confirm:** the server cannot see it and does not need it. D2b holds the test that the Delete button asks first. D2b also maps the HTTP detail of a refusal into its `ok`, `message` and `data` (`readRefusal` in `lib/studio/formModel.ts`). **Deploy.** The code ships dark. With the key unset, no write is direct. `scripts/vps_apply.sh` copies `STUDIO_DIRECT_KEY` from `.env` to `.env.local` and never makes one. It reads the last line of the key, as systemd and dotenv do. `.env.example` documents it. Setting the key on a box is gate `env-write`. Generate it on the box with `openssl rand -hex 32`, then restart `acb-gateway` and `acb-workbench`. **Known limit.** Skills run in the gateway process, so in-process code could read the key. The fence `test_only_the_proof_module_and_settings_read_the_direct_key` and review stop that. | `test_studio_direct_proof.py` (no database): the proof round trip, the window, the key rules, the shared vector with TypeScript, the deploy line, and the fences. `test_studio_action_door.py` (no database): the proof cases, the body keys, the error map and the writer cap. `test_studio_action_door_r8.py` (R8, `_DB_GATE`, `--must-run`): a valid proof writes one record and one audit row in one transaction. A failed audit leaves no record. A call with the bearer and the member email but no proof writes nothing. A proof for another email, path or time writes nothing. Constraint, move and formula refusals write nothing and keep the audit row. Three busy writers get 503 and the fourth is refused at once. Vitest: `src/app/api/studio` and `src/lib/gateway.test.ts`. Each mutation in the docstring of `test_studio_direct_proof.py` turns a named test red | HIGH | AGENT-SAFE for code. The key on a box is env-write |
| **B2c-2a** | 🔨 **BUILT, dark (2026-11-06).** **Delegated writes at the door** (§20.4, task 1336). B2c-2 elsewhere in this spec means the four rows B2c-2a to B2c-2d. A delegated write is a call of an object create, update or delete that is not direct. Mira, a workflow, a skill or an outside agent starts it. **B2c-2a calls the approval store of MCP-P1b-1 and builds no second one.** `DELEGATED_EXECUTORS` holds the three object writes. **Dark.** While `APP_REQUESTS_ORGS` leaves the org out (`app_requests.requests_allowed`), each delegated write gets 403 `approval_required`, as before. The slice adds no flag. **The door delegates only an explicit call (fix round 1).** A call with no proof opens a card only when its query holds the ask marker `ask=1` (`invoke.ASK_MARKER`). It runs a request only when its query names a `request_id`. Each other call that is not direct keeps 403 `approval_required`, also while the org flag is on. So an unsigned Save of the member's own screen never gets a 202. The marker is in no digest and no binding. The tools of B2c-2b send it, and the member's screen never does. **The ask.** A delegated call with `ask=1` reads the body, and checks its keys as B2c-1 does. In the session of the second check, it then calls `gateway.app_requests.open_request`. The source and the app are `studio`, and the subject is the package id. The op is the action id, and the class comes from `OBJECT_OP_CLASSES`. The client stamp is `studio:door`. The server writes the card title from the verb and the object name, and the app name is the package name. The call is `invoke._door_call`: POST, the template `/studio/apps/{app_id}/actions/{action}`, the path, the query `[["uses", USES]]`, the body, and no `if_match`. USES is the `uses` of the action. **The open request (fix round 2).** `app_requests.open_studio_request` takes the lock of `open_request` for the member and the app first. Then it reads ONCE: the newest open request (awaiting, or approved and not spent, and live) of the binding `session:MEMBER:studio:PACKAGE_ID` and the digest of the call. A found request is the answer, so a retry gives it. Else the door opens a new request with a fresh key (`invoke._ask_key`: the digest and a nonce). So an ask after a close opens ONE new request. Two asks at once meet on the lock, and the second one finds the request of the first. The cost is one lock and one read, whatever the history of the change, and no cap applies. The read is a bounded index scan: `pm_agent_runs_app_member` (org, member, `created_at` DESC, WHERE app IS NOT NULL), with the window `created_at > now() - OPEN_WINDOW_SECONDS` (the life of a request and one hour) in the index condition. The replay of `open_request` does not change for its MCP callers. **The answer** is 202 `{state, request_id, expires_at}`, and nothing runs. The audit row has the outcome `asked` and the `request_id`, and it commits with the request. A body with the wrong keys is 422 `bad_input`, and an object that the package does not hold is 404 `object_missing`. Neither opens a card. A call over 32 KiB of JSON is 413 `payload_too_large`. **The resend.** The caller sends the action again with `?request_id=ID`. The door reads no byte of that body. It takes a writer slot of B2c-1 before the session, so a full org gets 503 `store_busy` with `Retry-After: 1`. In ONE `_tenant_session`, `invoke._run_spend` runs five steps in this order. First `read_request` gives the stored call. Then the door computes the digest of `_door_call` with the stored body. Then it calls `acb_auth.app_requests.spend`, runs the stored write with `_do_object_write`, and writes the audit row with `record_app_audit_in`. The spend and the write are in one SAVEPOINT. A write that does not run (a refusal, a missing record, a busy store) rolls the spend back, so the request stays approved. A failed audit rolls back the whole transaction. **What a request spends on.** The query holds the `uses` of the action. So a request spends only on its own package, member and action, and only while the action names the same object and verb. **A refused spend** answers by the reason of `classify_refusal`, each as `{code, message}`: 404 `request_not_found`, 403 `request_declined`, or 409 `request_awaiting`, `request_spent`, `request_expired` or `request_mismatch`. Each refusal names a step that works: send the action again with `ask=1`, or with the `request_id` after the approval. `request_mismatch` never says `ask=1`, because an ask finds the same approved request. It says to change the input, and names the known limit of a value of 1e16 or more. While the org flag holds, `approval_required` for a write of an object says to send the action again with `ask=1`. A dark org, a source that is not an object and a run with the mark keep the plain text. A refused write answers 422 `{code, message, rule, fieldErrors}`, as B2c-1 does. **The workbench.** `realDoor.mapAnswer` reads a 202 as `ok: false` with the sentence "This change waits for an approval, so it is not saved yet." So no block shows a waiting change as saved. **No person present (AUTO-7a).** A call with the mark of `acb_common.no_person` and with `ask=1` or a `request_id` gets 403 `no_person_present` after the first check, before the body read. It opens no card and spends no request. A call with the mark and with neither gets 403 `approval_required` at the first check, as each call with no marker does. Neither text says to send `ask=1`. The mark is a ContextVar, so it holds for an in-process caller only. An HTTP call does not carry it. **A direct call** keeps B2c-1 with no card, and the door does not read its `request_id`. **Not in this slice:** the chat tools of Mira and their resend (B2c-2b), the card in the chat (B2c-2c), a workflow node (B2c-2d), the door op, an integration, and the run of a published workflow. That run stays 403 `approval_required`, and no row owns it yet. **Known limits.** A body with a key that holds `token`, `secret`, `password`, `credential` or `api_key` always gets 422 `secret_in_payload` on the delegated path (`_holds_secret_key` of the store). The direct path writes it. JSONB gives back a float of 1e16 or more as an integer, so the digest of the stored call differs, and the resend gets 409 `request_mismatch`. | `test_studio_action_door_r8.py` (R8, `_DB_GATE`, `--must-run`), cases ab to ak. A delegated create, update and delete (B, B, C) each answer 202 and write nothing. A resend before the approval is 409. After the approval, the resend runs the stored call and reads no byte of its body. A second resend is 409 `request_spent`. A declined card writes nothing. A failed audit, a refused write and a busy store each roll the spend back. The 413, the 403 `no_person_present` and the dark 403 open no card. A request spends only on its own package, member, action and `uses`. Mutation D1 (skip the spend) turns each B and C case of the run test and of the decline test red. Mutation D2 (spend in a separate transaction) turns each B and C case of the failed-audit test red. `test_studio_action_door.py` (no database): the order and the one session of a resend, and the arguments of an ask. `test_no_person_present.py` names the door as a reader of the mark, and lists `/requests/{}/decision` in `_APPROVAL_ROUTES`. | **C1** | 🔨 **BUILT, dark (2026-11-01).** No route, no flag and no UI reach it. Migration `323_studio_objects.sql` copies the two `apps` blocks of 321 (PKG-4) byte for byte, so the two files apply in either order. **The typed object store** (D146.4, §3.1): an object registry, record tables on shared JSONB rows, the field types of §3.1, validation on each write, and an index budget per org. No per-tenant DDL. Expand-only migration, next free number (R1). **Built:** `acb_common/studio_fields.py` holds the 16 types and the one check of a spec and of a value. A2 imports `FIELD_TYPES` and `validate_field_spec` from it, and A2's `FieldDef` gained `hot`. `gateway/studio/objects_store.py` is the one writer. It holds define, add a field, turn a field hot or cold, and drop for the registry. It holds create, get, update and delete for one record. A read is by id only. Each record function takes a required `package_id`, so a door of one package cannot reach a record of another package. The caller owns the transaction, so C1b can join several calls. **The rules:** a hot field has one of eight types: text, select, number, money, date, datetime, checkbox or email. A hot field takes no `many`. A number is below 10^15 in size, and a value that is not zero is at least 1e-4 in size, because the check refuses a float in exponent form. `v_text` holds the first 256 characters of a text. The options of a select must differ by more than case. A URL holds no user name and no password. A stored field reads with no authoring check, so a stricter rule binds a new spec only. **The lock order:** every write takes the advisory lock of the org first, then the row lock of the object. The org lock is exclusive, so the writes of one org run one at a time. **Hard delete:** A5c-1 deletes a package. It deletes the records in chunks, and then the cascade from `apps` deletes the rest. The App Workshop delete (`routes/apps/lifecycle.py`) cannot reach a package. A5c-1 holds the confirm step: its preview names each object and its record count. **Not built:** no TypeScript twin of the value check. D2 adds it, with a parity test as A2 has | Each field type refuses a bad value with its field name. R8: FORCE RLS holds on each table, and org B reads nothing. A hot field gets an index, and no org passes its budget. `test_tenant_coverage.py` passes. **The budget:** at most 8 hot fields for each object and 24 for each org. The store refuses first, and the trigger `studio_object_fields_hot_budget` refuses too, also under two writers at once. A field turns hot only on an object of at most 50,000 records. A record holds at most 64 KiB (65,536 bytes) of JSON. **Tests:** `test_studio_fields.py` (each type, the spec rules, three fences). `test_studio_objects_r8.py` (`pytestmark = _DB_GATE`): FORCE RLS on all four tables, org B reads and writes nothing, each composite key refuses a row of another org, a hot row on each write, the 9th and the 25th hot field, two writers at once, the 64 KiB cap, the 50,000 cap, the cascades. `test_admin_member_purge.py`: no purge row and no cascade reaches a record | HIGH | AGENT-SAFE |
| **C1b** | 🔨 **BUILT, dark (2026-11-02).** Store level only: no route, no flag and no UI. B2b calls it later. **Multi-record writes and constraints.** One batch writes several records in one transaction, and a constraint runs on each write. **Migration:** `327_studio_object_constraints.sql` (R1: 325 and 326 are held by open pull requests, so check the number again at merge). Expand only: `studio_objects.constraints` is JSONB, NOT NULL DEFAULT `[]`, and a CHECK holds an array. No new table. **Ships:** `acb_common/studio_rules.py` holds the grammar and `RuleViolation`, with no SQL and no table name. `objects_store.py` gains `apply_writes`, `set_constraints` and the checks. `StudioObject` gains `constraints`. All SQL stays in `objects_store.py`. **Not shipped:** a manifest key (`rules` stays reserved, and C6 lifts it), state machines, formulas, constraints across objects or references, money fields, and a check of old data when a rule is declared. **The grammar.** `set_constraints(db, object_id, rules, *, package_id)` declares the rules of an object, and each call replaces the list. A rule has a `name` (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,47}$`, unique in the object), a `kind` and an optional `message` of at most 200 characters. An object holds at most 16 rules. Three kinds, and the list is closed. `range` is `{field, min?, max?}` on one number field of one record, and it needs one bound. Non-negative is `min` 0. A record with no value passes. A `range` field may be cold, because the check reads one record in Python. `unique` is `{fields: [<one hot field>]}`. An empty value never clashes. A text of 256 characters or more compares `data` too, because `v_text` holds 256. `group_sum_min` is `{field, group_by: [<1 or 2 hot fields>], min}`. It is the derived balance: the sum of a hot number field over the records with the same group values stays at least `min`. "Stock never below zero" is this kind over a ledger object. A record with no value in a group field joins no group. A key with no rows passes at any `min`, because a group that no longer exists holds nothing to break. So the last row of a group can leave. A key with rows and no amount sums to 0, and the floor judges it. A `unique` field and a group field have the type text, email, select, number, date or datetime. A checkbox has two states, and a money value has a currency, so neither is a key. Each named field must exist. A field of `unique` and of `group_sum_min` must be hot, and `set_constraints` refuses another and does not turn it hot. A field that a rule holds cannot turn cold while the rule stands, because a cold field has no hot rows to check. **A rule that the store cannot read fails closed.** A stored rule of an unknown kind, or a malformed one, does not stop a read. Each write of its object is refused until `set_constraints` replaces the list. Only raw SQL can store such a rule. **A declaration does not check old data.** A rule binds each write after it is declared. A `unique` key that clashes already is refused only when a later write touches that key. **On a floor of 0 or less, a write that does not lower a group never reads the group.** The store adds up the net change of the batch on each key of `group_sum_min`, in exact `Decimal`, from the values that it read under its locks. With a net change of zero or more, it skips the sum. The org lock makes that safe, and a group with no rows starts at a sum of 0, which meets the floor. So a receipt on a broken key passes and a repair is possible, and a write that lowers a broken key is refused. A floor above 0 always reads its group, because a new group starts below it. It holds from the first row: with `min` 1, a new group of 0.5 is refused, and a new group of 1 passes. **The batch.** `apply_writes(db, actor, *, package_id, writes)` answers one `Record` or `True` for each `Write`. A `Write` is a frozen dataclass: `op` (`create`, `update` or `delete`), `object_id`, `record_id` and `data`. A batch holds at most 50 writes, and a longer one is refused before any statement. **The lock order:** the org lock once, then the object rows FOR KEY SHARE in ascending object id, then the record rows FOR NO KEY UPDATE in ascending `(object_id, record_id)`. The store then applies the writes in list order. `set_constraints` takes the org lock, then the object row FOR UPDATE. **The wait.** `_lock_org` bounds each wait for the org lock at 1 s, for every caller, with a LOCAL `lock_timeout` in its own SAVEPOINT. A waiter past 1 s can try again, and the pool matters more. The old `lock_timeout` comes back as soon as the lock is held, so the bound covers the wait only. Past the bound the store raises `StoreBusy` (code `store_busy`): "This app is busy. Try again in a moment." It is retryable, the caller's transaction stays usable, and a route maps it to 503 with `Retry-After`. A deadlock that Postgres settles at this wait (SQLSTATE 40P01) raises the same `StoreBusy`. A deadlock on a later statement stays a database error. The one lock order is the `apps` row FOR UPDATE, then the org lock, and A5c-1 proves it on R8. Each waiter holds a pool connection for the whole wait, so the bound limits the delay of other tenants and does not end it. Measured on one PC, on a pool of 9 connections with one holder of the lock: a read of another org waits 1.1 s behind 12 writers, 3.2 s behind 30, 5.4 s behind 45 and 7.5 s behind 60. Behind 90 writers it fails with the pool `TimeoutError` at 10 s. **The check order:** apply all writes, then `range` on the last value of each written record, then `unique` and `group_sum_min` once for each touched key. So a stock move is judged on the net, and not on each row. A `group_sum_min` key is touched by the old and the new key of an update, by the key of a create and by the key of a delete. A `unique` key is touched by the new key only. A delete or an old key cannot add a clash, so a check there would only stop the repair of old duplicates. **Failure.** The whole batch runs in one SAVEPOINT. Any error rolls back the SAVEPOINT and raises `BatchError(index, cause)`, so the caller's transaction stays usable with its earlier work. A check reads hot rows only, through the query builders of C2a, in `_time_limit` (5 s). The one exception is a key of 256 characters or more. C2a has no hot candidates for it, so the check reads the records of the object, under the same deadline. A timeout refuses the batch with `query_timeout`. `create_record`, `update_record` and `delete_record` are a batch of one, with the same answers, so no single call skips a rule. C7b adds the member to `delete_record`, so a derived parent names who made the change. **The errors:** `ConstraintViolation(RuleViolation)` holds `.rule` and `.object`, and its message names the rule. It never echoes a value. `ConstraintError` refuses a declaration. `RecordNotFound` is a write that names no such object or record in the package. `StoreBusy` is the wait above, and a batch gives it as the `cause` of its `BatchError`. C6 and C7 reuse `RuleViolation` and `touched_keys`. **Concurrency.** Under READ COMMITTED, a check before a write is wrong unless the writers run one at a time. Every store write takes the exclusive org lock first, and each later statement gets a new snapshot. So all three kinds are correct against each other store writer. A database unique index is impossible, because no tenant gets DDL. ⚠️ **Known limit.** The org lock serializes all record writes of one org, and a finer lock is a later slice. A writer that skips the store is out of scope: raw SQL, and the cascade of a package hard delete. A write that lowers a group past about 500,000 rows can reach `query_timeout` (measured on one PC: 200,000 rows 1.5 s, 500,000 rows 4.2 s, 1,000,000 rows refused). The same limit binds each write on a floor above 0, because that floor always reads its group. A running balance table is a later slice. | `test_studio_rules.py` (pure) and `test_studio_objects_batch_r8.py` (R8, on the `--must-run` list). A move of 7 (stock 10) writes exactly 2 rows, and the reversed order passes too. The second write fails with a database error: the batch leaves 0 rows, an earlier row of the caller stays, and the session is usable. Stock 10, move -11: refused with `stock_not_negative` in the message, 0 rows written. Move -10 passes. Two real connections, stock 10, each takes 7, 10 rounds: exactly 1 success, 1 refusal, final sum 3. The same for `unique` on `sku`: exactly 1 row. Two writers with objects in opposite order, 20 rounds: no 40P01, with a timeout of 10 s. An update that moves a row to another group checks both groups. A delete of a positive row that breaks the balance is refused. 50 writes pass. 51 are refused with 0 statements run. A 17th rule is refused. FORCE RLS: org B reads no rule and no sum of org A, also with the same field keys. On 50,000 ledger rows the plan of a sum shows no Seq Scan and a bounded read. A timeout in a check refuses the batch and leaves the session usable. A waiter past 1 s gets `store_busy`, the session stays usable, and `lock_timeout` is back. A deadlock at the lock gives `store_busy` too. On the real pool, one holder and a full pool of waiters of org A leave a read of org B a connection. On a broken key, `-3` is refused and `+3` passes. A positive write on a group of 200,000 rows reads no sum. `9999-12-31` is a legal key. With `min` 1, a delete and a move of the last row pass, a new group of 0.5 is refused, and a new group of 1 passes. With `min` 1 and rows 5 and blank, a delete of the 5 is refused. A malformed stored rule refuses each write and breaks no read. **Verify:** `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_studio_rules.py tests/unit/test_studio_fields.py tests/unit/test_studio_query.py tests/unit/test_studio_manifest.py tests/unit/test_tenant_coverage.py`. With a private ladder database: `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_studio_objects_r8.py tests/unit/test_studio_query_r8.py tests/unit/test_studio_objects_batch_r8.py`. Then `ruff check`, mypy on the changed files, and `bash scripts/ci_local.sh`. | HIGH | AGENT-SAFE. After C2a |
| **C2a** | 🔨 **BUILT, dark (2026-11-01).** No route, no flag, no UI and no migration. **The object query:** filter, sort, paging, the cost guard and its errors. **Built:** `acb_common/studio_query.py` holds the grammar: the shape, the op table of each type and the caps. It holds no SQL and no gateway import. The list of B2b-1 and the chat tool pass their input through it, and `list_input_schema()` is the JSON Schema of that input. `objects_store.query_records(db, *, package_id, object_id, filter, sort, limit, offset)` is the one reader, beside the reads of C1. It answers None for an object of another package or org. `build_query` and `build_count` are pure. **The filter** is a list of at most 8 conditions `{field, op, value}`, and a record must meet each one. **The ops of each type:** text and email take `eq`, `ne`, `in`, `contains` and `is_empty`. Select takes `eq`, `ne`, `in` and `is_empty`. Number and money take `eq`, `ne`, `lt`, `lte`, `gt`, `gte`, `in` and `is_empty`. Date and datetime take `eq`, `lt`, `lte`, `gt`, `gte` and `is_empty`. Checkbox takes `eq`, `ne` and `is_empty`. Another type and a field with `many` take no filter. The grammar refuses a range op on text. **The values** go through `studio_fields.check_value`, so an email compares in lower case and a select value must be an option. `in` takes at most 100 values. A money filter compares the minor amount, and it does not check the currency. A date value means the whole day in UTC. So `lte` a day is up to the end of the day, and `gt` a day is after its end. Apart from the checkbox rule, `ne` matches only a record that holds a value, and only `is_empty` (true or false) matches a record with no value. An empty text is a value. **The checkbox rule (the one exception).** The build loop decided this rule on 2026-11-02, and the owner can change it. A checkbox has two states for a member. `eq true` and `ne false` match a ticked record. `eq false` and `ne true` match an unticked record and a record with no value, because a form that does not send an unticked box writes no value. `is_empty` matches only a record with no value. **The 256-character rule:** `v_text` holds the first 256 characters. So `eq`, `ne` and `in` with a value of 256 characters or more check `data` too. `contains` checks `data` when `v_text` holds 256 characters. **The sort:** one hot field, then the record id. A text sorts by its first 256 characters. A record with no value of the sort field comes after the others, in the ascending order of its id. A checkbox field takes no sort, because `v_bool` has no index. With no sort, the order is the record id. **Paging:** `paging.bounds` gives a page of 25 records by default and 100 at most. The offset is at most 10,000. The total is exact below 10,001. At 10,001 the page gives `total_at_least` in its place. `page_line` gives the line, and it names no offset past 10,000. **One statement for a page (fix round 1).** One statement reads the page and its total, so one snapshot serves both. A write between two reads cannot put a record on a page twice. For a sort, the head reads the hot index of the sort field in order. The CTE `hc` counts the head up to the end of the page. So the tail reads only the rows that the page needs, and no rows when the head fills the page. A Sort node takes at most the rows of one page. **The candidates.** Each part of the statement reads its candidates from the hot rows of one hot condition (a time or a text match first). With no such condition, it reads the records of the object by the records index, in the order of the id. Each other condition tests the candidate id. A condition that reads `data` runs after a lookup of the record by its primary key. **The cost guard**, in three layers. (a) The caps of the shape. (b) `hot_or_small`: the grammar refuses a sort on a field that is not hot. A filter on a field that is not hot reads `data`, so it runs only when the hot filters leave at most 5,000 records. The page statement holds this count as the CTE `g`, so a write between two statements cannot pass the guard. Then the MATERIALIZED CTE `m` holds those candidates once, and `data` is read only for them. The count and the page read `m`. (c) One call has one deadline of 5 seconds. Each statement of the call, from the read of the object on, runs in a SAVEPOINT. Before each statement, the store sets the LOCAL `statement_timeout` to the time that is left. With no time left, it refuses with `query_timeout` and runs no more statements. **A field that turns cold.** The page statement counts the fields that it reads as hot and that are still hot (the CTE `v`). A field that turned cold after the store read the fields has lost its hot rows. So the store reads the fields again and runs the query once more. A second change gives `query_timeout` with the sentence "The fields of this object changed during the query. Try again." A timeout rolls back the SAVEPOINT only. The caller's transaction stays usable, and the old timeout comes back. **The six errors:** `QueryError` holds `query_field_unknown`, `query_op_not_allowed`, `query_value_invalid`, `query_not_indexed`, `query_too_big` or `query_timeout`, and one sentence. The sentence never echoes a value. A route answers 503 for `query_timeout` and 422 for each other code. **Injection:** a field key and a value are always binds. The SQL text takes a column name and an operator from a constant map only. ⚠️ **The known gap:** no `numeric` comparison is LEAKPROOF in Postgres 16. So the app role gets no index condition on `v_num`, and a number or money filter reads the hot rows of its one field (`docs/rls_and_indexes.md`). A sort on a number still reads the index in order. A checkbox filter reads the hot rows of its field too. The verifier measured a number range filter at 2.3 seconds on 600,000 records. No cap limits the records of an object after a field turns hot. A later slice fixes the gap with a migration. **Not built:** group, count, sum and average (C2b). B2b-1 gives the query its route | `test_studio_query_r8.py` (`pytestmark = _DB_GATE`, in the `--must-run` list). Each op of each type gives the same records on a hot field and on a field that is not hot. `ne` skips a record with no value, and `lt` and `lte` differ at the boundary. A long text matches against `data`. A sort pages each record once, with the ties in id order and the empty records last. A query of another package, object or org reads nothing. The store refuses a filter on a field that is not hot over 5,000 records. At a 1 ms timeout the query gives `query_timeout`. Then `get_record` runs in the same session, and the old timeout is back. On 50,000 records, EXPLAIN of the SQL of `build_query` shows the hot index, no Seq Scan, and no Sort node for a hot sort. A one-day filter (1%) reads at most 2,500 hot rows. A cold filter after a hot filter reads at most 5,000 records, and no count or page scans a table. A second session that clears a value between two reads puts no record on the page twice. A slow statement leaves less time for the next one. Each checkbox op gives the same records on a hot field and on its cold twin. A field that turns cold during a call gives the right page. A bulk insert during a call cannot pass the guard. `test_studio_query.py` (no database): the op table, each refusal, the caps and the injection fences. No migration. **Not in C6:** a role guard waits for C8 (a declaration with a role is refused, and the message says so). A history row waits for C4. An expression, a money guard and a read of another record wait for C7. **Where it is declared.** The manifest is the one source. An object gets a `rules` list beside `fields`, and C6 lifts `rules` from `RESERVED_KEYS`. `state_machine` and `states` stay refused. The store keeps the list in `studio_objects.constraints` as a fourth kind, `state_machine`, so A5 can pass the manifest list unchanged to `set_constraints`. The manifest check calls `parse_rules` and `check_rules_fit` of `studio_rules.py`, the one parser. **The grammar.** `{name, kind: "state_machine", field, initial, moves: [{from, to, guard?}]}`. `field` is one select, not `many`, and it may be cold. `initial`, each `from` and each `to` are options of the select. A machine has 1 to 64 moves and no move twice. An object holds one machine for each field, and at most 16 rules (the C1b cap). A guard is a list of 1 to 4 atoms, and all must hold. An atom is `{field, op, value?}` with the ops eq, ne, lt, lte, gt, gte, in, is_empty and not_empty. A guard reads the merged record only. It never reads another record, another object or the clock. The ops of a type are a subset of the C2a table (`OPS_BY_TYPE`), plus not_empty. A money field takes no guard yet, and a datetime field takes no order op. **Enforcement.** `_check_records` of `objects_store.py` judges each written record from `mark.before` and `mark.after`, under the same org lock and SAVEPOINT, with no new SQL. A create takes `initial` when the field is absent, and another value is refused. An update that changes the value needs a matching move and a guard that holds. An unchanged value passes. A cleared field is refused. A stored state that is not an option is refused as the start of a move. An unchanged one passes, so an edit of another field can still save a record after an option goes. A guard value must equal the stored form of its field. A batch judges the net move of each record, so draft, then approved, then sent counts as draft to sent. A delete is not a move and has no check. A stored machine that the parser cannot read fails closed, as a stored rule of an unknown kind does. `touched_keys` answers no key and `hot_keys` answers none for a state machine, so the batch queues no SQL key check for it. **The error.** `MoveViolation(RuleViolation)` holds `rule`, `object`, `from_state` and `to_state`. The store computes each formula inside `_apply_write`: it validates the record without its targets, then runs each formula in dependency order, then builds the hot rows from the result. A read never computes. **Grammar:** literals (`num` as decimal text, `money`, `text`, `bool`, `date`), `ref` to a field of the same record, and the functions by type. Null propagates, and `coalesce` and `is_empty` take it. `if` is lazy. Caps: depth 8, 64 nodes, 4 args, 256 steps, 8 formula rules for each object. **Money:** whole minor units, no float, a named `rounding` (`half_up`, `half_even`, `down`, `up`) on each `div` and on each `mul` with money. Division by zero is `division_by_zero`. **Number precision:** a number target has a `scale` (0 to 4) and a `rounding`. The stored result holds at most 15 significant digits at its scale: `abs(result) < 10 ** (15 - scale)`, or the write is refused with `result_too_large`. So a JSON float holds it exactly. Money stays a whole number of minor units below 10^15. **Targets:** number, money, text, checkbox, date. A target is not `required`, and a caller cannot write it. **The description.** `StudioObject.computed` and the `computed` part of `studio_describe("fields")` name each formula target as computed, so a Form shows it read-only and sends no key for it. A write that holds a key for a target is refused with `computed_field`, also with the same value. **Declare first.** A new formula, or a formula with a changed `target`, `expr`, `scale`, `rounding` or `currency`, on an object that holds records is refused (`ConstraintError`, "declare before the first record"), because there is no backfill. A change of only `name` or `message` passes. ⚠️ A stored formula that is malformed counts as a new one. The author removes it first, and can add it again only with C7c. A removed formula is allowed, and its stored values stay as plain values. **The other rules.** `range`, `unique`, `group_sum_min` and a state-machine guard that name a formula target read the stored result, because the checks run on the record after the formulas. `rule_reads(rule)` answers every field key that a rule reads, and C9 uses it. **Errors:** `FormulaError(RuleViolation)` holds the rule, the object and a code (`division_by_zero`, `currency_mismatch`, `result_too_large`, `step_cap`, `type_error`, `bad_argument`, `bad_result`, `computed_field`, `declaration`). It never echoes a value. A tree over a cap is refused at declaration with the JSON path and one sentence, and a forged stored tree refuses each write. A forged stored list of more than 8 formulas refuses each create and update with `declaration`, and a delete computes nothing, so it passes. A create stores the result and an update recomputes it from the merged record. A write of a target is refused, also with the same value. A tie rounds as the named mode, positive and negative. A zero divisor refuses the batch with 0 rows written and the session usable. A tree over a cap is refused at declaration with a JSON path. A forged stored tree refuses each write. The BFF signs the direct proof for EVERY same-origin POST to the action route, so it cannot tell a read from a write: this client guard is the only line. The door maps the verb to the action id that the manifest declares (`{id, uses}`), because the route takes the id. It flattens each record of the list answer to a row of `id` plus `data`. `run` maps a 422 (`code`, `message`, `rule`, `fieldErrors` as a list) to `data` with `fieldErrors` as a map, `rule` and `message` (`readRefusal` in `formModel.ts`), a 503 to a retry sentence with the `Retry-After` wait, and a 403 `approval_required` to a plain sentence. It never shows the raw error text. (2) Inline edit in the Table for the scalar types (text, number, select, checkbox, date, url, email, phone), in the store `lib/studio/inlineEdit.ts` on the pattern of `boardMove.ts`, drawn by `components/studio/StudioTable.tsx` through an optional `edit` prop of `DataGrid`. The cell shows the new value at once, one update follows with the id and one key, and a refusal restores the old value and shows the rule or field sentence. A field that a `formula` or `rollup` rule fills never edits. Other types edit in the Form. (3) The Form creates and updates through the real door, with no change to `StudioForm`. (4) `components/studio/DeleteRecordButton.tsx` on `ui/ConfirmDialog`, in the Detail sheet, with the store `lib/studio/deleteRecord.ts`. (5) The preview route `/studio/package-preview?pkg=<id>&object=<name>`, in no nav pane: a package picker, an object picker, then the Table, the New-record Form and the Detail sheet. A package with no object shows "Publish the package first". An org outside `STUDIO_ORGS` gets an empty state and no error. (6) Chat READ tools `studio_package_get` and `studio_record_list` in the studio pack, with their `chat_parity.py` rows (the studio ceiling stays 0). (7) `scripts/dev_seed_studio.py` makes and publishes a sample package with three records on the local stack. `dev_up.sh` passes `STUDIO_DIRECT_KEY` to the gateway and the web app only when the operator sets `DEV_UP_STUDIO_DIRECT_KEY` in the shell. **Fix round 1 (2026-11-04):** Two guards keep a read from writing. The client guard holds for the manifest that the page read at open. A later draft can point an action id at a write. So each `.get` body is `{id, read: true}`, and every write refuses a body with that key (`_write_input` takes exactly the keys of the write). Both Forms leave out a field that a `formula` or `rollup` rule fills, because the store refuses it with 422 `computed_field`. The page keeps one door while the package and its actions stay the same, so a focus read does not reset the edit store or the delete flow. A failed read shows one alert with the sentence of the door, and Retry shows only for a 503 or a network fault. A rule refusal shows only the own message of the rule after its name. An editable cell wears a small pencil mark that needs no hover. The seed holds a `line` object with a formula, `total = qty * price`, and no record, because a formula must exist before the first record. **Not in this slice:** a chat write tool and any agent write (B2c-2), a view-to-node mapper and a sidebar entry (PKG-8, H1), a nav entry, a Board change, a batch route, a delete in the Table row, a gateway route, a change to `route.ts` or `lib/gateway.ts`, and setting `STUDIO_DIRECT_KEY` on a box (gate `env-write`). | **Done when:** a published package shows its real records in the Table. An inline edit sends one update and the new value stays after a refresh. A refused update restores the old value and shows the rule or field sentence. The Form creates a record and shows a 422 under its field. Delete asks first, and Cancel sends nothing. Mount, focus and refresh of the Table send no write. `test_chat_parity` passes with the studio ceiling at 0. **Merge condition:** the owner accepts, in the PR thread, that a write from chat waits for B2c-2, and a task blocked by B2c-2 records it. **Fences:** `realDoor.test.ts` (the read guard, mount/focus/refresh send no write, the flat rows, the 422/503/403 map), `inlineEdit.test.ts` (a refused update restores), `DeleteRecordButton.test.ts` (Delete asks first), `StudioTable.test.ts`, `package-preview/page.test.ts` (the route gate), and in Python `test_studio_record_tools.py` (read-only tools, one frame, a declared list action only), `test_chat_parity.py`, `test_tool_packs_registry.py`. Each test names its mutation in its docstring. | STD | AGENT-SAFE, with the UI gate |

The drag is native HTML5, as on the other three boards. No Projects code changes, and `src/lib/boards.test.ts` lists the new file. The pure model is `lib/studio/boardModel.ts` (no React import). The view is `components/studio/StudioBoard.tsx`. `bindsSource` in `dataDoor.tsx` gains `board`. **Shape:** `{name: "board", source, data: {fields, groupBy, titleField, cardFields, update, maxCards}}`. `groupBy` must name a select field, or the block shows an error state. A lane holds one option, in the option order. A record with no value or an unknown value goes to a last lane, "No value". `cardFields` holds at most 3 fields, worded by `displayValue` and drawn by `ValueElement`, so no new formatter or chip exists. Each lane shows at most `maxCards` cards (default 50) and a "Show more (n)" line. The count chip shows the true count. **Drag contract:** the card moves at the drop, and exactly one `door.run(update, {id, data: {[groupBy]: option}})` runs. A drop on the same lane calls nothing. A card with a call in flight is locked, so two drops make one call. A refusal puts the card back at its lane and index, and the board shows the door message in a `role="alert"` line through `readRefusal`. A rule refusal names the rule. The keyboard path: Space picks a card up, the arrows choose a lane, Space drops it, Escape cancels, and an `aria-live` line says the result. The greying waits for the C6 state machine to publish a move table. **Live half:** a drag on a stored object runs on the real door since D3b (below). The option order holds. 60 records at cap 50 show 50 and "Show more (10)", and the count chip shows 60. `StudioBoard.test.ts` (the vitest config includes `*.test.ts` only): a drop on a new lane makes exactly 1 `run` call with `{id, data: {status}}`. A same-lane drop makes 0. Two drops while one call is pending make 1. A refused update puts the card back in its lane, and the alert text equals the door message. The keyboard path makes 1 call. A `groupBy` that is not a select gives the error state. `dataDoor.test.ts` covers `board`. `boards.test.ts` lists the new file. A source check holds the block to `displayValue`. | **F1b** | 🔨 **BUILT, dark (2026-11-05): F1b-1.** F1b-2 and F1b-3 are open. **Hidden characters in a skill** (Metorite task 1555). A model reads a skill as instructions. So a character that no person sees can carry a second text into the prompt. **F1b-1** (this slice): the store refuses such a text, and the chat tools defang the read of an old row. The store does not strip in silence, because a strip changes what the owner saved and breaks the trust of `content_hash` and `based_on`. **One rule:** `packages/acb_skills/acb_skills/hidden_text.py`, with `find_hidden`, `strip_hidden`, `hidden_problem` and `body_hidden_problem`. It builds on `reply_card.is_invisible_char`: the Unicode table `Default_Ignorable_Code_Point`, U+2800, U+1D159 and category C (each control, format, surrogate, private use and unassigned code point). A new line, a tab and a carriage return are legal. U+200C and U+200D are legal between two visible characters, and a legal U+FE0E or U+FE0F counts as visible on the left. At a word end (the end of the text, or white space that a person sees), ONE joiner is legal directly after a virama (canonical combining class 9). A legacy input writes a Malayalam chillu and a Bengali khanda ta as a virama and a ZWJ, and an explicit halant as a virama and a ZWNJ. The rule refuses each other joiner: at the start, next to white space, next to a second joiner, or at a word end after any character but a virama. It refuses a Persian ZWNJ before a space, because the space already breaks the join, so that ZWNJ changes no shape. U+FE0E and U+FE0F are legal only directly after one visible symbol of category S, after one of the five emoji bases outside category S (U+203C, U+2049, U+2139, U+3030, U+303D), or after a keycap base with U+20E3 next. The rule refuses two in a row, and a selector after other punctuation, such as a period. **The store** (`skills_store._text` and `check_body`) refuses the name, the description and each string and each key of the body. **The 422 text** is one sentence: "PATH: This text holds a hidden character at position N (U+CODE). Remove it." It never echoes the text. **The route** answers 422 to a publish or a resume of a stored body, name or description with a hidden character. A pause stays open. No route renames a skill, so a skill with a hidden name cannot go live again, and the member makes a new skill. **The chat tools:** a read with an id leaves each hidden character out of the description, the instructions and the actions. It does this before the frame, the cut and the counts. The read gives a `hidden` count for each field and one note, and the tool refuses a save of such a field. A list leaves them out of the name. A save sends the stored body as it is, so the store names an old field in its 422. The checks of a held save and of a publish call the same functions, so no card shows for a text that the store or the route refuses. The notes of a read and of a refused save say that each save from chat sends the stored text back, so the member replaces the whole text in Studio first. The gateway reads stay raw, because the F2b editor must show the mark. **Cost, accepted:** the LRM and RLM marks, the Arabic marks U+0600 to U+0605 and U+06DD, the Japanese ideographic variation sequences and the tag flags (England, Scotland, Wales) fail the rule. So does a character newer than the Unicode of the runtime (Python 3.12 has Unicode 15.0), such as the emoji U+1FAE9, because the runtime reads it as unassigned. Plain Arabic, Hebrew and Japanese text works. **The rule takes, by choice:** NBSP and the other Zs spaces, U+2028 and U+2029, long runs of marks, and look-alike words of mixed scripts. **Residual risk:** each gap between two visible characters can hold nothing, a ZWJ or a ZWNJ, which is about 1.6 bits. A visible symbol adds about 1.6 bits (nothing, U+FE0E or U+FE0F), and so does a virama at a word end. So 1,000 characters of plain text can carry about 200 bytes. The rule bounds the channel to these places but cannot close it. **No migration:** the trigger `studio_skill_versions_immutable` refuses each UPDATE of a version, and the pack is dark. **Follow-ups:** F1b-2 (task 1681) adds the mark in the editor and the `skillErrors.ts` rule, with an owner preview. F1b-3 (task 1682) runs after G1c and MCP-P1b-1 merge: the card helpers of `studio_tools.py` and `gateway/app_requests.py` call `hidden_text`, and the dead smuggle code goes. The card count of the tools (`_shown_count`) still flags a legal ZWJ or U+FE0F as hidden, and F1b-3 moves it to `hidden_text`. Task 1683 converges the other rules of an invisible character | (1) `create_skill` and `add_version` raise `SkillStoreError`, and `POST /studio/skills` and `POST /studio/skills/{id}/versions` answer 422, for a hidden character in the name, the description, `body.description`, `body.instructions`, any nested body string and any body key. The detail names the path and the position and never echoes the text. (2) A ZWNJ in Persian, a ZWJ in Hindi and Bengali, a family emoji, a heart with U+FE0F, a keycap, the emoji U+2139 and U+3030 with U+FE0F, and a joiner after a virama at a word end (Malayalam, Bengali, Devanagari) save and read back byte-equal. (3) The store refuses a TAG character, U+E0100, U+200B, U+202E, U+FEFF, U+00AD, U+3164, U+2800 and a C1 control. It also refuses a joiner at an edge, a double joiner and a letter with U+FE0F after it. (4) `PATCH` of the state to `published` answers 422 for a stored body, name or description with a hidden character. (5) `studio_skill_list` on an old row returns text with no hidden character and a `hidden` count for each field. The tool refuses a save of that field. (6) A chat save or publish shows no card for refused text and returns the sentence of the route unchanged. (7) `skills_store.py` and `studio_tools.py` hold no list of invisible code points of their own (a test reads their source), but the names that F1b-3 removes. Its result is the exact input of `ask_questions`, which stays the one ask seam. `studio_plan` checks the manifest and shows one card with `request_confirmation`. The card draws each name from the manifest inside « », and the member rejects it to build nothing. The model cannot call that function, so the tool calls it and returns a `decision`: `approved`, `revised`, `stopped` or `no_card`. `stopped` means the card closed with no approval: a Reject, a timeout or an answer by another member. `no_card` means the chat shows no card. The plan text stays in `context`, and the member answers in words. A child agent never holds `studio_plan` (it is in `DISPLAY_TOOLS`). The card shows objects, views, workflows, skill, connections and actions. "Ask only what the brief does not say" is model judgement, and it is **advisory**. The build cost is **not priced yet**: no credit estimator exists, and J3 sets the caps. The card shows the count of parts and the line "Build cost: not priced yet. Credit limits come with a later release." It shows no price and no turn count | **Enforced:** one `studio_interview` call holds at most five questions, one topic each (`test_studio_interview_caps_at_five_questions`). **Advisory:** the limit across a whole request (one interview, one plan card, no direct `ask_questions` call) is a rule for Mira and no code counts it. A counter per run is a follow-up. An invalid manifest shows no card. A stop on the card writes nothing: `studio_plan` makes no write call and G1 keeps no store. After a stop, the rules forbid `studio_patch` (advisory). Fences: `test_studio_interview_caps_at_five_questions`, `test_studio_interview_refuses_repeat_topic`, `test_a_stop_writes_nothing_so_any_non_get_call_fails`. Verify: `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_studio_interview_plan.py tests/unit/test_studio_builder_tools.py tests/unit/test_tool_packs_registry.py -q` | STD | AGENT-SAFE |

Stateless and read-only, like G1 | **Built:** `part` is `manifest`, `fields` or `blocks`. A bad part gives `ok: false` and the legal parts. The tool reads `Manifest.model_json_schema()`, `studio_fields` and `BLOCKS` live and copies none. It adds no per-block prop docs. The pack now holds eight tools. The schema it returns is the one A2 checks against, from the same source. A test fails when the two differ. Each result stays under G1's cap | STD | AGENT-SAFE. Stage 2 |
| **G1c** | 🔨 **BUILT, dark (2026-11-04).** **Chat saves and publishes** (Metorite task 1445). Scope (narrowed, 2026-11-04): before G1c, no chat tool wrote a package store. **Files:** in `packages/acb_skills/acb_skills/studio_tools.py`, a third tuple `STUDIO_PACKAGE_TOOLS` holds two tools. With the two record tools of D2b, the `studio` pack holds 15 tools. `agents.py`, `config.json` and `instructions.md` of `agent-studio-builder`, `skill_families.py` and `tool_packs.py` (`_STUDIO_RULES`) follow. **Tools:** `studio_save(manifest, package_id="", based_on="")` sends `POST /studio/packages`, or with an id `PUT /studio/packages/{id}/draft`. `studio_publish(package_id, notes="")` sends `POST /studio/packages/{id}/versions`. Both call the A5 routes by HTTP as the member (`_send`). Each tool parses the id as a UUID before it builds a path. No tool takes an org, a member, a visibility or a confirm argument. `studio_list_actions` gains `app=""`, a UUID that goes as `?app=`, and the route then gives only the rows of that package. **The hash:** `acb_common.studio_manifest.manifest_hash` is the sha256 of the canonical JSON (sorted keys, no space, each zero float as `0.0`), and it is the ONE hash of a draft: the tools and the package store import it. The store keeps a manifest as `jsonb` in its own key order. The R8 suite proves that the stored manifest equals the sent manifest, and that the two hash equal. A jsonb number has no sign of zero, so the hash writes `-0.0` as `0.0` (fix round 2, R8 test g). **Proof of the plan:** `studio_plan` writes a tenant-Redis record only when the card answer is True. The key holds a hash of the member of the run and a hash of the manifest id, and the value holds the manifest hash and the time of the approval. So a member holds one approval of an app, the newest. The TTL is 2 hours (`PLAN_NAMESPACE`, the key builder of `tenant_redis`). The org and the member come from the run. A plan card that the member stopped or revised writes a withdraw stamp for the member, and its result says so (fix round 2). One read takes the record and the stamp, and a record from before the stamp gives no plan, for each app of the member. So a stop on a plan with a new manifest id also withdraws the approval of the old id. A plan approved after the stamp still publishes. When the stamp write fails, the process remembers the withdraw, and its publish refuses. `studio_publish` reads the stored draft, hashes it, and reads the key. A missing record, a Redis fault, a run with no org and a changed draft each refuse with no publish request. **Fix round 1 (2026-11-04):** the tool calls of one model response run at once, so a save could land between the read of a publish and its POST, and the new draft went live on the old approval. Now the publish sends the hash that the record matched as `based_on`, and a save by id sends the hash that it read. The store compares each under its row lock (A5), and a different hash is 409 `draft_changed` with no write. After a 201 the tool deletes the record when it still names the published draft, so one approval buys one publish. The read and the delete are two calls, because the tenant Redis wrapper has no EVAL and no WATCH. A newer approval that lands between them is lost, and its publish asks for a new plan card. A `draft_changed` refusal names its recovery: a `studio_save` of the manifest with no `package_id` answers with the `based_on` of the stored draft. **Cards:** in a clear chat a save writes at once, and a publish shows no second card, because the plan card approved this manifest by its hash. In a chat where `egress.write_must_show_card()` is true, a save and a publish each show one card from `card_body`: the outline, the count of parts, the line of the tables (`structure_line`: the fields, the hot fields, the required fields and the rules), the size and the first 12 hex digits of the hash. **The plan card** shows the line of the tables too. Its last line says that approval saves and publishes the app with no other card, and that its objects become tables in the organization that no member can remove yet. In a held chat the last line says that a save and a publish each show one more card. A publish card adds the version and the notes. A card that cannot fit refuses, and a held chat refuses a cut card (`chat_is_held`, `cut_refusal`). A decision of `revised`, `stopped` or `no_card` sends nothing. After `approved`, the tool reads the draft again and sends nothing when it moved. Before a held card, the tool runs the checks of the route that need no call: A2 with no catalog for a save, and A2 with the catalog of `catalog_rows` and the notes checks of the store for a publish. It also checks the fixed manifest id, a manifest id that the member holds, and the limit of 100 packages. **Size:** the tool refuses a manifest over 24,000 characters before any call. No result holds stored manifest text. A save result gives the id, the outline, `live_version` and the new `based_on`. A save with an id needs `based_on`, and a missing or different value refuses with no write. A manifest equal to the stored draft writes nothing, so a second try after a network miss is safe. Chat never replaces a stored draft over the cap. A 409 `package_exists` gives the id, the outline of the stored draft and `based_on`. A stored draft over the cap gives the outline only and `truncated: true`. **Problems:** a 409 `objects_out_of_sync` gives at most 20 errors and a count of the rest. Each error has a path by index, and a message of the kind, the names inside « » and a fixed fix line for each of the seven kinds. A name holds at most 36 characters and no invisible character. The text of the server never reaches the model, and the note says that the names are data. A 422 gives the message and each error of the route unchanged. A 503 says "Nothing was published. Try again." A network miss says that the act may have worked, and that a second try is safe. **Non-goals:** list, get and versions tools (G1d), rollback, delete (A5c), share, any UI file, any `chat_parity.py` row (no UI sends these routes), any edit of `egress.py` or `HELD_WRITE_OWNERS`, the production `TOOL_PACKS` line and the `STUDIO_ORGS` switch. **Residual:** the publish route takes no expected hash, so a small window stays between the read of the draft and the publish. Only the same member can use it. **Ships dark:** the pack is empty for an org outside `STUDIO_ORGS`, which is empty on every box. | `tests/unit/test_studio_chat_build_r8.py` (R8, in the `--must-run` list of `pr-check.yml`) passes on a private database. The stored manifest hashes equal to the sent manifest. A member asks for a visit log and approves the plan, and the package, its version row and its object and field rows exist. A publish with no approved plan sends no request and makes no version row. A manifest that drops a field answers 409 in the framed form, with no new version row and no new object row. The tool refuses a draft that changed after its plan. Fix round 1: a save of a new draft and a publish in one response, also with a plan card of the new draft that the member rejects, publish the planned draft or nothing, in 8 of 8 rounds (`test_r8_a_save_and_a_publish_at_once_never_publish_the_new_draft`, `test_r8_a_rejected_plan_in_the_same_response_never_publishes_the_new_draft`). `tests/unit/test_studio_package_tools.py` passes. It is hermetic: a fake gateway reads each body with the real A5 parse and store checks and each path with the real router, and keeps a manifest as `jsonb` does. It holds the twelve fences of the audit, and its docstring records 16 mutation runs with their red tests. `test_tool_packs_registry.py`, `test_pack_requests.py` and `test_studio_interview_plan.py` pass with their edits. `test_chat_parity.py`, `test_pack_guards.py`, `test_read_tool_paging.py`, `test_own_tool_scope_parity.py`, `test_pack_cues.py`, `test_held_card_promises.py`, `test_card_body.py`, `test_pack_no_double_write.py`, `test_studio_skill_tools.py` and `test_studio_builder_tools.py` pass with no edit. **Verify:** `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_studio_package_tools.py tests/unit/test_studio_skill_tools.py tests/unit/test_studio_builder_tools.py tests/unit/test_studio_interview_plan.py tests/unit/test_tool_packs_registry.py tests/unit/test_pack_requests.py tests/unit/test_pack_guards.py tests/unit/test_read_tool_paging.py tests/unit/test_own_tool_scope_parity.py tests/unit/test_chat_parity.py -q`, the R8 file on a private database, ruff, and `bash scripts/ci_local.sh` | HIGH | AGENT-SAFE. | **BETA-2** | 🔨 **BUILT, dark (2026-11-06).** **The chat builder adds the record actions** (task 1810). The app page (Table, Form, Board) reads and writes an object only through plain `object:<name>.<verb>` actions. A plain action has no `set`, because the web door skips each action with a `set`. The chat tool `studio_record_list` refuses with no list action. A chat-built app could open to an empty screen. **The rule:** (a) each object gets a plain `list` and a plain `get` when it has none. ### Group H — The shell

**Since D147, H1 and H2 run on the package-host registry.** `package_host.md` owns the registry, the scopes, the lifetimes and the slots. PKG-8 moves H1 and H2 onto it, and J2, J3 and M1 share its install path (PKG-9 to PKG-12).

| Id | What | Done when | Tier | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **H1** | **Carried by WS-PKG PKG-8** (`package_host.md`, D147). Installed apps in the sidebar, per org, through the one run-time registry. Studio builds no list of its own. **PKG-8a is built dark (2026-11-07):** a publish keeps a private sidebar entry of the owner at `/apps/<package id>` (`package_host.md` §5.5). | **O20** | **One team inbox** (task 2424). Wave 2 (D202.7). The threads of the org's WhatsApp number (O6) and of its shared mailboxes (task 733) in one queue. A thread gets an assignee and a linked record. It builds on the ticket queue of E21 (task 2202) and the shared mailbox, and adds no second inbox seam | A WhatsApp thread and a mail thread show in one queue. An assignment tells one member. A member sees only the threads of the mailboxes and the numbers that they may open | HIGH | AGENT-SAFE. After task 733, O6 and E21 (task 2202). The audit of O20 names the one seam that it extends |
| **O21** | **A sender for an org with no channel** (task 2425). Wave 2 of §20.8 (D202.7). A sender subdomain that Metorite gives, for notices only, as a fallback. Metorite gives no shared WhatsApp number | An org with no domain sends a booking notice from the subdomain. The send path refuses a marketing send from it. Each org keeps its own suppression list (CRM-M11) | HIGH | AGENT-SAFE build (D202.7, question C). A live send is an OWNER-GATE (CLAUDE.md §3a rule 3) |

| **1 · Studio opens** (M1, dark) | A1, A2, B1, D1, E1, F1, F2a, F2b, F2c, G1 | One Studio pane in preview, the skill creator for private skills, the catalog, and the manifest | None. D146 answered STU-Q1 and Q2 |
| **2 · First apps** (M2) | A3, A4, A5, B1c, B2a, B2b, B2c-1, B2c-2a to B2c-2d, C1, C1b, C2a, C2b, C6, C7, C8, D2, D3, D4, D11, D12, F3, F5, F10, G1c, G2, G3, G4, G5, H1, J1, J2, K1, K3 | Build, preview and publish an S2 app in chat. | **3 · Automations and rich blocks** | B1b, B4, B6, B7, C3, C4, C5, C9, C10, C11, D5 to D9, D13, E2 to E6, E8, F4, F6, F7, H2, I1, I2, I3, I4, J3, J4, J6, K4, K5 | S1 and S3 in full, connector apps, calendar, timeline, mind map, playbooks, first-party logic and the MRP-lite template | H-328 for unattended runs. WS-67 for F3 |
| **4 · Code, outside agents, scale** | B3, B5, B8, C12, D10, F8, F9, G6, G7, H3, K2, L1, L2, M1 | Member logic modules, the code block, MCP access, pinned answers | MCP-P1b, SBX-9, CA-14 (#1042), MCP-P5 (#869) |
| **5 · Outside world** | O1 to O14, with CRM-M8 and CRM-M11 | Public forms and kiosks, WhatsApp flows, payments and bookings, apps over any outside system, and insights (§20) | Stage 2 apps live. STU-Q16 to STU-Q23. H-328 and B6 for unattended runs |

**The three waves of D202.** The use-case brainstorm of task 2326 ordered the new rows in three waves, and the owner answered "looks good" (D202.5). The waves map onto the stages as this table shows. Each row of §14 names its wave.

| Wave | What a member gets | Stage | New rows | Starts after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1 · Trackers, registers and procedures, with no code** | Line items, template and run, the guided step player, photo, signature and scan fields, auto-number, SLA timers, the Knowledge link, record history and the version lock, Mira inside each app, and AI fills that a person confirms | Stage 3, first | C13, C14, C15, C16, C17 (with C4), C21, D14, D19, D22, D24, D25, E9, F11, I5, B9, B10, B11, then the templates K6, K7 and K8 | Stage 2 delivers its first apps (A5, B2c-1, D2b, D11b, PKG-8, F10). D4 to D11 finish first |
| **2 · Documents and reach** | Print layouts (D13), versioned documents with e-signatures, offline capture, translation, video to steps, the 3D viewer, generated images, the channel rows, git save history, and the blocks and the template of the clinic study | Stage 3, second. O15 to O21 join Stage 5 | C18, C19, C20, C22, C23, C24, C25, C26, D16, D17, D18, D20, D21, D23, D26, D27, D30, E10, E11, E12, G14, G17, H4, B12, B13, B14, B15, J8, J9, J10, O15 to O21, and the clinic rows B17, D31, I6 and K9 (D202.8) | Wave 1. Group O keeps its own order (§20.8) |
| **3 · Code and extension** | The full code IDE with the UI kit and custom screens, the diagram canvas, 3D scenes, then server code after SBX-9 | Stage 4 | L3 first, then J7, L4, G8, G16, L1, L5, L6, G9, G10, G11, G12, G13, G15, G18, G19, G20 to G26, D15, D28, D29. After SBX-9: B5, B8, L7, B16, G7 | L3 passes. If L3 fails, every code row waits for SBX-9 (D202.4) |

**Rows outside the waves.** B18, I7 and K10 serve HR, and the owner put them later on 2026-11-08 (D202.7). G27, the VS Code extension, comes later too (D202.10). `package_host.md` PKG-14c is the slot row of D202.7, in Phase P3 of that spec.

**The first dispatchable slice is B1.** It waits on no owner question, writes nothing, and every later group reads it.
